     1	//! Authoring-only source spans. Never part of the schema, IR or device payload.
     2	use crate::{ast::Expr, lexer::{Kind, Token}};
     3	use std::collections::BTreeMap;
     4	
     5	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
     6	pub struct SourceMap {
     7	    expressions: BTreeMap<String, (Expr, String, bool, crate::Site)>,
     8	    pub(crate) declarations: BTreeMap<String, String>,
     9	    pub(crate) repairs: Vec<crate::repair::Candidate>,
    10	}
    11	
    12	impl SourceMap {
    13	    pub(crate) fn extend(&mut self, other: &Self) {
    14	        self.expressions.extend(other.expressions.clone());
    15	        self.declarations.extend(other.declarations.clone());
    16	    }
    17	
    18	    pub(crate) fn record(&mut self, expr: &Expr, lines: &[String], tokens: &[Token]) {
    19	        // Block expressions consume layout tokens after their final source
    20	        // token. A dedent's site can already be on the next declaration.
    21	        let Some(end) = tokens.iter().rposition(|t| !matches!(t.kind, Kind::Newline | Kind::Indent | Kind::Dedent | Kind::Eof)) else { return };
    22	        let tokens = &tokens[..=end];
    23	        let Some(first) = tokens.first() else { return };
    24	        let Some(last) = tokens.last() else { return };
    25	        let Some(line) = lines.get(last.site.line as usize - 1) else { return };
    26	        let tail: Vec<_> = line.chars().skip(last.site.col as usize - 1).collect();
    27	        let mut length = last.value.chars().count();
    28	        if last.kind == Kind::Str {
    29	            length = 1;
    30	            while length < tail.len() {
    31	                let c = tail[length];
    32	                length += 1;
    33	                if c == tail[0] { break; }
    34	                if c == '\\' { length += 1; }
    35	            }
    36	        }
    37	        let mut text = String::new();
    38	        for number in first.site.line..=last.site.line {
    39	            let Some(line) = lines.get(number as usize - 1) else { return };
    40	            if number != first.site.line { text.push('\n'); }
    41	            let start = if number == first.site.line { first.site.col as usize - 1 } else { 0 };
    42	            let end = if number == last.site.line { last.site.col as usize - 1 + length } else { line.chars().count() };
    43	            text.extend(line.chars().skip(start).take(end.saturating_sub(start)));
    44	        }
    45	        let mut depth = 0_i32;
    46	        let grouped = first.kind == Kind::Symbol && first.value == "(" && tokens.iter().enumerate().all(|(i, t)| {
    47	            if t.kind == Kind::Symbol {
    48	                if t.value == "(" { depth += 1; }
    49	                if t.value == ")" { depth -= 1; }
    50	            }
    51	            depth > 0 || i + 1 == tokens.len()
    52	        }) && depth == 0;
    53	        self.expressions.insert(expr.site().to_string(), (expr.clone(), text, grouped, first.site.clone()));
    54	    }
    55	
    56	    pub(crate) fn get(&self, expr: &Expr) -> Option<(String, bool)> {
    57	        let (syntax, text, grouped, _) = self.expressions.get(&expr.site().to_string())?;
    58	        // A planner may reuse a site while changing the expression.
    59	        (syntax == expr).then(|| (text.clone(), *grouped))
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    pub(crate) fn read(&self, site: &crate::Site) -> Option<(crate::ast::ReadExpr, String, bool, crate::Site)> {
    63	        let (expr, text, grouped, start) = self.expressions.get(&site.to_string())?;
    64	        if let Expr::Read(read) = expr { return Some((read.clone(), text.clone(), *grouped, start.clone())); }
    65	        // Keyed write diagnostics share the read repair verifier. Replace only
    66	        // the target; keep the write kind, condition and payload in its caller.
    67	        let Expr::Write { table, keys, site, .. } = expr else { return None; };
    68	        let tokens = crate::lexer::lex("", text).0;
    69	        let table_token = tokens.get(1)?;
    70	        let mut depth = 0;
    71	        let close = tokens.iter().skip(2).find(|token| {
    72	            if token.kind != Kind::Symbol { return false; }
    73	            match token.value.as_str() { "[" => depth += 1, "]" => { depth -= 1; if depth == 0 { return true; } }, _ => {} }
    74	            false
    75	        })?;
    76	        let offset = |site: &crate::Site| {
    77	            let lines = text.split_inclusive('\n').collect::<Vec<_>>();
    78	            let line = site.line.checked_sub(1)? as usize;
    79	            Some(lines.get(..line)?.iter().map(|s| s.len()).sum::<usize>() + lines.get(line)?.char_indices()
    80	                .map(|(i, _)| i).nth(site.col.checked_sub(1)? as usize)?)
    81	        };
    82	        let from = offset(&table_token.site)?;
    83	        let end = offset(&close.site)? + 1;
    84	        let mut target_site = start.clone();
    85	        target_site.line += table_token.site.line - 1;
    86	        target_site.col = if table_token.site.line == 1 { start.col + table_token.site.col - 1 } else { table_token.site.col };
    87	        Some((crate::ast::ReadExpr { table: table.clone(), keys: keys.clone(), site: site.clone(), index: None,
    88	            cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new() },
    89	            text[from..end].into(), false, target_site))
    90	    }
    91	
    92	    pub(crate) fn expression(&self, expr: &Expr) -> String {
    93	        crate::print::with_source(expr, &|expr| self.get(expr))
    94	    }
    95	
    96	    /// A comparison's operand needs parentheses around booleans and conditionals.
    97	    pub(crate) fn operand(&self, expr: &Expr) -> String {
    98	        crate::print::with_source_at(expr, 5, &|expr| self.get(expr))
    99	    }
   100	}

   173	
   174	pub fn programs(
   175	    _catalog: &Catalog,
   176	    schema: &mut Schema,
   177	    programs: &[BoundProgram],
   178	    diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>,
   179	) {
   180	    for bound in programs {
   181	        let mut fact = schema.operations[&bound.program.name].clone();
   182	        if let Err(message) =
   183	            snapback4_core::schema::ephemeral::execution(schema, &bound.program, &mut fact)
   184	        {
   185	            let (message, fix) = message.split_once("; ").unwrap_or((
   186	                &message,
   187	                "call a separate ephemeral mutation from the client",
   188	            ));
   189	            diagnostics.push(
   190	                Diagnostic::new(&bound.site, "E_QUARRY_EPHEMERAL", message, fix).guide("ephemeral"),
   191	            );
   192	        }
   193	        fact.search_decoration = snapback4_core::search_decoration::admit(schema, &bound.program).is_ok();
   194	        fact.search = snapback4_core::search_plan::ordinary::composition(schema, &bound.program);
   195	        let merges = snapback4_core::ir::body_merges(&bound.program.body);
   196	        fact.branch_merge = merges.iter().any(|m| m.branches());
   197	        fact.branch_pages_v2 = merges.iter().any(|merge| merge.branches() && merge.lanes.iter().any(|lane|
   198	            lane.guarded() || lane.scan.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(predicate_probe)
   199	            || schema.table(&lane.scan.table).is_some_and(|table|
   200	                lane.grant() && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(snapback4_core::schema::Sync::is_union)
   201	                || !snapback4_core::engine::rules::membership_point_id(schema, lane) && table.index(&lane.scan.index).is_some_and(|index| lane.scan.prefix.len() == index.components.len()))));
   202	        // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#17 — older validators cannot prove a fixed second witness field.
   203	        fact.branch_pages_v3 = merges.iter().flat_map(|merge| &merge.lanes).any(|lane| schema.table(&lane.scan.table).is_some_and(|table| lane.membership() && (multi_field_probe(&table.rules.read) || table.leave == snapback4_core::schema::LeavePolicy::DeliveredHistory)
   204	                || (lane.guarded() || lane.grant()) && negative_probe(&table.rules.read)));
   205	        fact.branch_pages_v3 |= snapback4_core::ir::scans(&bound.program).iter().any(|scan|
   206	            snapback4_core::engine::rules::page_guard_decision(schema, scan).as_ref().is_some_and(negative_probe));
   207	        fact.branch_pages_v3 |= bound.program.body.iter().any(uncapped_statement);
   208	        // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — PAGE5's fixed residual point needs the new validator.
   209	        fact.branch_pages_v4 = merges.iter().flat_map(|merge| &merge.lanes).any(|lane|
   210	            lane.vacancy() || lane.membership() && lane.scan.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(predicate_probe));
   211	        fact.branch_membership = merges.iter().any(|merge| merge.lanes.iter().any(|lane| lane.membership()));
   212	        fact.branch_membership_v2 = merges.iter().flat_map(|merge| &merge.lanes)
   213	            .any(|lane| membership_v2(schema, lane));
   214	        schema.operations.insert(bound.program.name.clone(), fact);
   215	    }
   216	}
   217	
   218	fn predicate_probe(expr: &snapback4_core::ir::Expr) -> bool {
   219	    use snapback4_core::ir::Expr;
   220	    match expr {
   221	        Expr::Exists(_) | Expr::Get(_) => true,
   222	        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => predicate_probe(left) || predicate_probe(right),
   223	        Expr::Unary { value, .. } => predicate_probe(value),
   224	        Expr::Field { base, .. } => predicate_probe(base),
   225	        Expr::Array(values) => values.iter().any(predicate_probe),
   265	fn negative_probe(rule: &snapback4_core::schema::RuleExpr) -> bool {
   266	    use snapback4_core::schema::RuleExpr;
   267	    match rule { RuleExpr::Not(inner) => matches!(inner.as_ref(), RuleExpr::Exists { .. }),
   268	        RuleExpr::And(terms) | RuleExpr::Or(terms) => terms.iter().any(negative_probe), _ => false }
   269	}
   270	
   271	fn uncapped_expr(expr: &snapback4_core::ir::Expr) -> bool {
   272	    use snapback4_core::ir::{Builtin, Expr};
   273	    match expr {
   274	        Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::RequireUncapped, .. } => true,
   275	        Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::JobQuery { program, .. }, args } =>
   276	            program.body.iter().any(uncapped_statement) || args.iter().any(uncapped_expr),
   277	        Expr::Builtin { args, .. } | Expr::Array(args) => args.iter().any(uncapped_expr),
   278	        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => uncapped_expr(left) || uncapped_expr(right),
   279	        Expr::Unary { value, .. } | Expr::Field { base: value, .. } => uncapped_expr(value),
   280	        Expr::Block { body, value } => body.iter().any(uncapped_statement) || uncapped_expr(value),
   281	        Expr::Object(fields) => fields.values().any(uncapped_expr),
   282	        Expr::Shape { base, fields } => uncapped_expr(base) || fields.values().any(uncapped_expr),
   283	        Expr::Get(read) | Expr::Exists(read) => read.key.iter().any(uncapped_expr),
   284	        Expr::Scan(scan) => uncapped_scan(scan),
   285	        Expr::Merge(merge) => merge.lanes.iter().any(|lane|
   286	            uncapped_scan(&lane.scan) || lane.setup.iter().any(uncapped_statement)
   287	            || lane.guard.as_ref().is_some_and(uncapped_expr) || lane.project.as_ref().is_some_and(uncapped_expr)
   288	            || lane.source.as_ref().is_some_and(|source| source.scan().is_some_and(uncapped_scan) || source.expressions().into_iter().any(uncapped_expr))),
   289	        _ => false,
   290	    }
   291	}
   292	fn uncapped_statement(stmt: &snapback4_core::ir::Stmt) -> bool {
   293	    use snapback4_core::ir::{Stmt, LoopDomain};
   294	    match stmt {
   295	        Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Push { value, .. }
   296	        | Stmt::Return(value) | Stmt::Require { condition: value, .. } => uncapped_expr(value),
   297	        Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => uncapped_expr(condition)
   298	            || then_body.iter().any(uncapped_statement) || else_body.iter().any(uncapped_statement),
   299	        Stmt::For { domain, body, .. } => body.iter().any(uncapped_statement)
   300	            || match domain { LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value, .. } => uncapped_expr(value), LoopDomain::Page(scan) => uncapped_scan(scan), _ => false },
   301	        _ => false,
   302	    }
   303	}
   304	
   305	fn uncapped_scan(scan: &snapback4_core::ir::Scan) -> bool {
   306	    scan.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(uncapped_expr) || scan.after.as_deref().is_some_and(uncapped_expr)
   307	        || scan.before.as_deref().is_some_and(uncapped_expr)
   308	        || scan.prefix.iter().filter_map(|key| key.scalar()).any(uncapped_expr)
   309	}

# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,

   705	## 14. PAGE: capped owner-or-membership pages
   706	
   707	PAGE resolves Q6 for the common rule
   708	
   709	```quarry
   710	read <- .owner = viewer or exists members[.parent, viewer]
   711	```
   712	
   713	when the membership lookup names an exact unique key and the membership table
   714	declares a finite cap whose grouping columns are fixed by the viewer or other
   715	row-independent expressions. More formally, the read rule's top-level
   716	disjunction has at least one principal-party equality and at least one such
   717	membership probe, and every disjunct is one of those two forms. Every
   718	qualifying probe has one row-dependent exact-key component naming an ordinary
   719	target column (not `id`) that the page has not already pinned, and otherwise
   720	fixed components; its cap group is wholly among the fixed components and its
   721	read rule is decided true by them. The §2–§3 exclusions still apply: total
   722	page predicates, shared order and total cursor suffix, portable keys and
   723	labels, at most eight physical components in both source and target indexes,
   724	and fallback without speculative indexes. The planner first lowers
   725	bare booleans and negated booleans to equality pins, as it does for every
   726	other intent plan. It emits the party lanes, one descriptor per membership
   727	probe, and one system lane. Their static descriptor count remains at most
   728	eight; each declared membership cap bounds its descriptor's dynamic fanout
   729	separately and may itself be greater than eight.
   730	
   731	The dynamic descriptor scans the membership range to completion before the
   732	page may emit a row. Each distinct parent in that result opens a target range
   733	pinned to that parent. The membership row is the witness for the rule branch,
   734	so target rows in that range need no second membership probe; their ordinary
   735	target coverage, horizon and expiry checks still apply. The owner and parent
   736	ranges merge in the requested order and `id`, deduplicate overlaps, and share
   737	one version-4/5 position. Source discovery and every opened target scan use
   738	the query's one store transaction/snapshot, so a membership cannot be revoked
   739	between proving a lane and reading it. The system lane reads the whole
   740	original range and suppresses every other lane for a system context.
   741	
   742	The cursor descriptor includes the static lanes and the membership source's
   743	table and index. Its scope includes the evaluated static pins and the source
   744	range's fixed viewer/argument pins. It deliberately does not digest the set of
  1030	cuts before every lane opens. The language, simulator and both device stores
  1031	walk each form through one shared cursor and test overlap de-duplication.

  1071	## 16. PAGE3: finish the ordinary private-page plans
  1072	
  1073	Commissioned 2026-10-13; implemented locally. Muse and fresh Codex both
  1074	READY in round 5 at `65d505289f`; verbatim reviews and frozen brief are
  1075	`llp/reviews/3000.036-page3.r5.{muse,codex,brief}.md`.
  1076	PAGE2 left 18 notes in 11 of the 50 odd wave-10 final schemas: 12
  1077	party/static-grant pages, three compound rules, two hidden parent sources
  1078	and one pure membership. This amendment extends the existing plan rather
  1079	than asking builders to merge lists themselves. It changes no read authority,
  1080	replicated data or runtime resource ceiling.
  1081	
  1082	**Static grants and whole-group reads.** An owner-or-readable-static-grant
  1083	rule uses the existing party/grant merge on synced union tables too. The
  1084	server evaluates the positive readable grant once; its holder reads the
  1085	original whole range, everyone else the party ranges. An explicit `first` or
  1086	`last` is a page bound, not a table cap: a role holder may page an unbounded
  1087	table, under the usual request meter. An implicit whole-group read is expanded
  1088	only if its original fixed prefix has an applicable stored cap or fully
  1089	pinned total unique, at or below the examined ceiling. That original bound
  1090	applies to the union. A cap on owner does not bound a staff's whole table.
  1091	A fully pinned total unique may supply a one-row lane with the implicit id
  1092	position, even when its physical index has no unpinned component.
  1093	
  1094	**Full union devices.** The same descriptor and scope serve server and device
  1095	cursors. On a device a full union target's grant lane reads its held authorized
  1096	rows, with the ordinary acquisition, coverage and meter checks; it does not
  1097	probe a remote grant. That held range covers every other target lane, so the
  1098	others are not opened. Missing acquisition stays unknown. Online-only and
  1099	single-arm narrowed sync retain their existing grant deferral. This amends
  1100	LLP 3000.036.000.000 §5.3's planner restriction, while preserving its holding
  1101	and acquisition invariants.
  1102	
  1103	**Pure membership.** Remove the syntactic party requirement from §15. A rule
  1104	`exists members[.id, viewer]` or `exists members[.parent, viewer]` has a system
  1105	range and exact membership descriptors, no party descriptors. All source
  1106	readability, unique/cap proofs, total filters, index/key bounds, shared
  1107	positions and discovery/head fences remain. If the source discovery is cut,
  1108	no target rows or cursor are emitted. An empty complete discovery means an
  1109	empty complete page. Point uniqueness still does not cap memberships.
  1110	
  1111	**Positive compound rules.** Distribute positive `and`/`or` into at most seven
  1112	alternatives plus the original system range. Each alternative retains all
  1113	of its row equality conditions. It contains either one exact membership
  1114	witness, or row equality pins and positive row-independent guards. A membership
  1115	conjunct supplies precisely its own authority; all remaining conjuncts must
  1116	be true by the target lane's pins. A pinned membership target instead uses
  1117	a readable page guard, without discovering hidden source rows. No negated
  1118	probe, arithmetic, fallible predicate or second varying source component is
  1119	admitted. Candidates are speculative and fall back when the physical plan
  1120	or bound-IR validator cannot prove the whole admitted shape.
  1121	
  1122	A new guarded-range descriptor evaluates `page_guard_decision` on its own
  1123	pins. A true readable guard opens only that range, not the other alternatives;
  1124	a false guard opens none. It does not cover other lanes. The system range
  1125	covers every lane only for the system. Status/party lanes, discovered parent
  1126	lanes and guarded lanes share one ordered id-tied position and deduplicate
  1127	there, as in §§3–6. Thus if `R = ∨i (Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi)` and W is the total page
  1128	predicate, each lane returns exactly `W ∧ Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi`; their union is W ∧ R.
  1129	Guard facts and membership sources must be readable, as in §15, so neither
  1130	empty results nor completeness reveal hidden witness rows. Read-meter cuts
  1131	and frontier rules are unchanged; heap exhaustion remains a refusal.
  1132	
  1133	A nullable small enum's `field != null` has a finite positive expansion into
  1134	its declared non-null values. This retains null exclusion exactly; ordinary
  1135	nullable inequalities against other values keep their original scan. The
  1136	wave-10 archive with four species therefore gets four enum ranges plus its
  1137	owner, readable reviewer and system ranges. Compound volunteer-open and
  1138	parent-teacher/status rules preserve each status condition when opening lanes.

# Snapback 4 journal

Append-only, dated. What actually happened against LLP 3000 §5's plan. Never
rewrite an entry; correct it with a later one. Decisions that must outlive this
file go to LLP 3000 §7.

## 2026-09-12 — the clock starts

- Author accepted the bakeoff (LLP 3000 decisions 1–8) and said "go".
- LLP 2000, 2000.000 and 3000 landed on `main` as `638b74fb9f`, with the
  Snapback 2 substrate freeze in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`.
- This commit is the fence: `snapback4/` with its own Cargo and Bun workspaces,
  the card contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), a binary that only
  knows `--version` and `doctor`, `scripts/test.sh`, this journal, the
  not-doing list, and the root routing lines. The four-week clock runs from
  this commit's landing; its SHA is recorded in the next entry.
- Lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4`, branch `sb4/root`. Toolchain: Rust 1.97.0
  (pinned), Node 24.18.0, Bun as installed at the root.
- Decision taken today: the simulator is written in Rust and hosts the Rust
  server core; whether it hosts the client through a Rust client core or
  through an embedded JS engine running the TypeScript client is decided with
  the week-3 byte measurement (LLP 2000.000 §9). Recorded in LLP 3000 §7 when
  the skeleton lands.
- Decision taken today: the Snapback 2 corpus and `ir-contract.json` are copied
  in week 1 (they are the contract any interpreter is held to); the Rust
  engine pieces are copied in week 2 as the engine needs them, split on the
  way in, rather than wholesale now.
- Not yet measured: the five floors (LLP 3000 §5, week 1). Next entries.

## 2026-09-12 — the fence landed; the five floors

- The fence is `961a3eb1c5` on `main`. The four-week clock runs from it:
  the first slice is due on the hub fixture by 2026-10-10.
- Toolchain (author, same day): `tsc` to typecheck, `rolldown` to bundle and
  to measure bytes, `oxc` to transform and minify; esbuild only with a reason
  written here first. Recorded in LLP 3000 §7.
- The Snapback 2 corpus (295 cases) and `ir-contract.json` are copied to
  `corpus/`; nothing reads them yet.

### The physical floors (LLP 3000 §5, week 1)

Machine: Apple M5 Max, 128 GB, macOS 26.6.2; Node 24.18.0 (`node:sqlite`,
bundled SQLite); Chromium from the Playwright cache (`chromium-1243`), headless.

99,090 messages across 901 conversations, about 204 bytes per row as a

   682	            "public" => Ok(RuleExpr::Public { reason: format!("{} is public", info.decl.name) }),
   683	            "true" => Ok(RuleExpr::Allow),
   684	            "false" => Ok(RuleExpr::Deny),
   685	            other => Err(unknown(site, format!("`{other}` is not a rule; rules are allow, deny, public, exists, created, and comparisons over ., viewer and literals"), "read <- .ownerId = viewer")),

   690	                _ => format!("{} is public", info.decl.name),
   691	            };
   692	            Ok(RuleExpr::Public { reason })
   693	        }
   694	        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } => match op.as_str() {
   695	            "and" | "or" => {
   696	                let left = rule_expr(catalog, info, left, bindings)?;
   697	                let mut guarded = bindings.clone();
   698	                guarded.present.extend(present_terms(&left, op == "and"));
   699	                let right = rule_expr(catalog, info, right, &guarded)?;
   700	                Ok(if op == "and" { RuleExpr::And(vec![left, right]) } else { RuleExpr::Or(vec![left, right]) })
   701	            }
   702	            "=" | "!=" | ">=" | ">" | "<=" | "<" => {
   703	                let ordered = !matches!(op.as_str(), "=" | "!=");

   488	fn union_refusal(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, sync: &SyncDecl, rule: &RuleExpr, source: &str) -> Diagnostic {
   489	    sync_error(&sync.site, format!("sync cannot infer an audience from `{source}`: a synced table's read rule must be `public`, `.column = viewer`, `exists target[.columns, viewer]`, or an `or` of whole `.column = viewer` disjuncts and at most one `exists target[.columns, viewer]`, so each device holds every row its viewer may read and no other; do not make private rows public to get past this"), audience_split(catalog, info, rule))

   491	
   492	// @ref LLP 3000.007#6.6 — omitted audiences invert exactly the same three read rules.
   493	fn infer_audience(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, sync: &SyncDecl, audience: &RuleExpr, rule: &RuleExpr, source: &str, named: Option<&str>) -> Fallible<Audience> {
   494	    let refusal = || union_refusal(catalog, info, sync, rule, source);
   495	    match audience {
   496	        RuleExpr::Public { .. } | RuleExpr::Allow if named.is_none() => Ok(Audience::Public),

   502	            let recipient = probe.columns.get(key.len() - 1).ok_or_else(refusal)?;
   503	            let principals: Vec<_> = target.columns.iter().filter(|(_, ty)| ty.kind == TypeKind::Principal && !ty.optional).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).collect();
   504	            if principals.len() > 1 && named.is_none() {

   514	/// The paved splits that keep every reader (LLP 3000.036 §1 A), spelled

   516	/// (LLP 3000.036.000.000), so what reaches here is a branch that is not one
   517	/// audience: a shared (viewer-free) branch, or a branch with an extra
   518	/// condition or a literal key.
   519	// @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#2.2 — the refusal names the branch that is not a whole audience.
   520	fn audience_split(_catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, rule: &RuleExpr) -> String {
   521	    fn viewer(rule: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
   522	        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Viewer);
   523	        match rule {
   524	            RuleExpr::Public { .. } | RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Deny => false,
   525	            RuleExpr::Eq(l, r) | RuleExpr::Ne(l, r) | RuleExpr::Compare(_, l, r) => term(l) || term(r),
   526	            RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().any(viewer),
   527	            RuleExpr::Not(inner) => viewer(inner),
   528	            RuleExpr::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, t)| t)).any(term),
   529	        }
   530	    }
   531	    let whole = |rule: &RuleExpr| match rule {
   532	        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(_), RuleTerm::Viewer) | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(_)) => true,
   533	        RuleExpr::Exists { key, equalities, .. } => equalities.is_empty() && key.last() == Some(&RuleTerm::Viewer)
   534	            && key[..key.len() - 1].iter().all(|term| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(_))),
   535	        _ => false,
   536	    };
   537	    let table = &info.decl.name;
   538	    let audience = super::intent_sync::audience_rule(table, info, rule);
   539	    let online = "; or replace the sync line with `online only` and keep this rule";

   542	        let private = disjuncts.iter().find(|disjunct| viewer(disjunct)).map_or_else(|| ".owner = viewer".to_owned(), |disjunct| disjunct.quarry());
   543	        return format!("keep shared and private rows in two tables: the shared rows in a `public '..'` table with its own sync, the rest in a table read and synced by `{private}`, moving a row when it becomes shared (guide sync){online}");
   544	    }
   545	    if disjuncts.len() > 1 && let Some(branch) = disjuncts.iter().find(|disjunct| !whole(disjunct)) {
   546	        let arms: Vec<String> = disjuncts.iter().filter(|disjunct| whole(disjunct)).map(|disjunct| disjunct.quarry()).collect();
   547	        return format!("each `or` branch of a synced read rule is one whole audience, `.column = viewer` or `exists group[.columns, viewer]`, and `{}` is not: keep its extra condition in the queries and mutations (`where ...`, `require ... else CODE`) and read under `{}`, which syncs as a union; or give its readers rows in a membership table synced to its members and read through it (guide sharing){online}",

   549	    }
   550	    format!("give each reader a row in a membership table synced to its members, and read through it: `table {table}Readers:` with `item: {table}`, `reader: principal`, `unique item, reader`, `immutable item, reader`, `read <- .reader = viewer`, `insert <- created {table}[.item] or exists {table}Readers[.item, viewer]`, `sync`; then here `read <- exists {table}Readers[.id, viewer]` and `sync to {table}Readers[.id] by .reader` (guide sharing){online}")
   551	}
   552	
   553	/// A rule stops binding at its first refused hop, so `check` printed one
   554	/// site per rule and builders fixed hops one run at a time (wave 7 lane 062).
   555	/// Each `and`/`or` operand is bound alone to find the rule's other hops,
   556	/// which the one diagnostic names (its rewrite already repairs every hop it
   557	/// can), and a table that reads through this one is named: its copy of the
   558	/// rule is checked only once this rule binds.
   559	// @ref LLP 3000.036#17.6 — every E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP site in one run.
   560	fn every_hop(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, rule: &RuleDecl, diagnostic: &mut Diagnostic) {
   561	    fn leaves<'a>(value: &'a Expr, out: &mut Vec<&'a Expr>) {
   562	        match value {
   563	            Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" || op == "or" => { leaves(left, out); leaves(right, out); }
   564	            other => out.push(other),
   565	        }
   566	    }
   567	    let mut parts = Vec::new();
   568	    leaves(&rule.value, &mut parts);
   569	    let mut more: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   570	    for part in parts {
   571	        let single = RuleDecl { value: part.clone(), ..rule.clone() };
   572	        if let Err(other) = bind_rule(catalog, info, &single) && other.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP" && other.site != diagnostic.site {
   573	            let text = crate::print::expression(part);
   574	            let text = text.strip_prefix('(').and_then(|t| t.strip_suffix(')')).map(str::to_owned).unwrap_or(text);
   575	            let entry = format!("{} (`{text}`)", other.site);
   576	            if !more.contains(&entry) { more.push(entry); }
   577	        }
   578	    }
   579	    if !more.is_empty() {
   580	        diagnostic.message = format!("{}; this {} rule hops at {} more site{} too: {} — the rewrite repairs every hop it names; repair the rest the same way", diagnostic.message, rule.effect.word(), more.len(), if more.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, more.join(", "));
   581	    }
   582	    if rule.effect == Effect::Read {
   583	        let children: Vec<&str> = catalog.tables.iter().filter(|(_, child)| child.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.parent == info.decl.name)).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).collect();

    60	/// The read rule as a sync audience: service grants removed, then each
    61	/// same-row `.principal = viewer` disjunct that a self-probe on a total
    62	/// unique key absorbs (the row is its own witness). The core's
    63	/// `sync_audience_rule` makes the same simplification and remains the authority.
    64	// @ref LLP 3000.036.000#3.1 — exact equivalence only; anything else stays a refused union.
    65	pub(super) fn audience_rule(name: &str, info: &TableInfo, rule: &RuleExpr) -> RuleExpr {
    66	    let audience = rule.device_audience_rule().into_owned();
    67	    let terms = audience.disjuncts();
    68	    if terms.len() < 2 { return audience; }
    69	    let absorbs = |disjunct: &RuleExpr, other: &RuleExpr| {
    70	        let (RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Viewer) | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(column))) = disjunct else { return false };
    71	        let RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } = other else { return false };
    72	        let Some(index) = info.indexes.iter().find(|candidate| &candidate.name == index) else { return false };
    73	        table == name && info.total_unique(index) && index.columns.len() == key.len() && index.columns.contains(column)
    74	            && equalities.iter().all(|(field, term)| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(source) if source == field) || (field == column && matches!(term, RuleTerm::Viewer)))
    75	            && index.columns.iter().zip(key).all(|(component, term)|
    76	                if component == column { matches!(term, RuleTerm::Viewer) } else { matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(field) if field == component) })
    77	    };
    78	    let mut kept: Vec<RuleExpr> = Vec::new();
    79	    for term in terms.iter().filter(|term| !terms.iter().any(|other| absorbs(term, other))) {
    80	        // A repeated branch is one audience, as the core folds it.
    81	        if !kept.iter().any(|seen| seen.normal_disjunct() == term.normal_disjunct()) { kept.push((*term).clone()); }
    82	    }
    83	    match kept.len() {
    84	        n if n == terms.len() => audience.clone(),
    85	        1 => kept.into_iter().next().expect("one audience"),
    86	        _ => RuleExpr::Or(kept),
    87	    }
    88	}

codex
I confirmed that the new index advice can rewrite an earlier, unrelated read in the same query. The fixed-grant checks also miss admissible cases when readability depends on a page pin or when fixed conjuncts are grouped together. I’m finishing the Lean cross-check for those cases.

   585	            diagnostic.message = format!("{}; {} read{} through {}, so {} copy of this rule is checked once it binds", diagnostic.message, children.join(", "), if children.len() == 1 { "s" } else { "" }, info.decl.name, if children.len() == 1 { "its" } else { "their" });
   586	        }
   587	    }
   588	}
   589	
   590	/// Bind a rule expression over a table's focal row.
   591	pub fn bind_rule(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, rule: &RuleDecl) -> Fallible<RuleExpr> {
   592	    let bindings = RuleBindings { old: rule.bindings.first().cloned(), next: rule.bindings.get(1).cloned(), update: rule.effect == Effect::Update, insert: rule.effect == Effect::Insert, hops: rule.effect != Effect::Read, present: Vec::new() };
   593	    rule_expr(catalog, info, &rule.value, &bindings).map_err(|error| {
   594	        let mut error = super::first_paste::inherited_rule(catalog, info, rule).unwrap_or(error);
   595	        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR" && rule.effect == Effect::Update && rule.bindings.is_empty()
   596	            && error.rewrite == "write this as a named transition" {
   597	            let source = format!("update (old, next) <- {}", crate::print::expression(&rule.value));
   598	            error.rewrite = format!("name both row images: `{source}`");
   599	            error.source_rewrite = Some(source);
   600	            error.source_rewrite_kind = Some(crate::diag::SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
   601	        }
   602	        // @ref LLP 3000.036#13.6 — the named form of a whole unnamed update rule, so the rewrite keeps every other clause.
   603	        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR" && rule.effect == Effect::Update && rule.bindings.is_empty() && error.rewrite.starts_with("name both images") {
   604	            let comparison = error.message.split('`').nth(1).unwrap_or_default().to_owned();
   605	            error.rewrite = super::rule_operator::named_update(&info.decl.name, &rule.value, &comparison);
   606	        }
   607	        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP" {
   608	            error.rewrite = match rule.effect {
   609	                Effect::Read => reference::read_hop_rewrite(catalog, info, &rule.value).unwrap_or_else(|| "a read rule probes one hop: copy the deciding value onto this row (immutable, checked against the parent by the insert rule), or use read through .parent".into()),
   610	                _ => reference::reference_rewrite(catalog, info, &rule.value),
   611	            };
   612	        }
   613	        // @ref LLP 3000.036#14.3 — a read-through child binds its parent's rule over hidden
   614	        // captures (`.$enrollment.dancer`): its refusal is the parent's (`internal_names` drops the copy).
   615	        // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.6 — a `read through` that did not expand (E_READ_THROUGH says why)
   616	        // leaves its internal call; its refusal is that problem's cascade, spelled as written.
   617	        if let Expr::Call { name, args, .. } = &rule.value && name == "$readThrough" {
   618	            let reference = match args.as_slice() { [Expr::Name { name, .. }] => name.clone(), _ => "parent".into() };
   619	            error.message = format!("`read through .{reference}` did not expand, so {} has no read rule yet", info.decl.name);
   620	            // The cascade convention: `check` hides it beside its cause unless --verbose.
   621	            error.rewrite = "fix this Quarry line and rerun snapback4 check".into();
   622	            error.cascade_of = Some(rule.site.clone());
   623	            error.mechanical = false;
   624	            return error;
   625	        }
   626	        if rule.effect == Effect::Read && let Some(through) = &info.through {
   627	            error.rewrite = format!("repair {}'s read rule (its own diagnostic prints the repair); {} reads through it", through.parent, info.decl.name);
   628	            error.cascade_of = Some(error.site.clone());
   629	        }
   630	        error
   631	    })

VERDICT: NOT READY

- `f3e35c177b:snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs:797`: Fixed-probe readability uses the original rule key before substituting lane pins. For `roles` with `unique bucket` and `read <- .bucket = viewer`, `exists roles[.bucket]` remains rejected beside a varying membership even when the page pins `.bucket = viewer`. Prove readability using the substituted key while retaining exact authored-rule matching.

- `f3e35c177b:snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs:791`: Fixed-residual matching cannot discharge a compound rule sibling. `(fixedA and fixedB) and varying` falls back because the sibling `And` matches neither `rule_true` nor the leaf-only matcher; `varying and fixedA and fixedB` can succeed. Recursively establish all fixed conjuncts so grouping does not exclude admissible multiple-guard plans.

- `f3e35c177b:snapback4/lean/Quarry/Eval.lean:625`: The fixed-plus-varying classifier accepts exactly two non-pin atoms. A native-admitted rule `varying and fixedA and fixedB`, with both fixed sources readable, required, unique and timeless, receives an incomplete model verdict. The emitter supplies no unsupported boundary for this case. Classify exactly one varying witness plus all admissible fixed guards.

- `f3e35c177b:snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/mod.rs:992`: The equality repair replaces the first matching index text anywhere in the complete query declaration. If an earlier `logs` read also uses `by byStart`, advice for the later `watches` read changes `logs` to the newly declared watches index and leaves watches unchanged; the printed repair fails compilation. Rewrite the specific scan’s source span and validate the complete repair.
